| 2026-07-14 | [#72](https://github.com/Umbrella-IT-Group/metamcp/pull/72) | **Public-session TTL sweeper — structural fix for pool-cap saturation (pool-cap wave, Track A4)** — public endpoint (API-key/OAuth) StreamableHTTP sessions are created per request stream and only torn down on a client-sent `DELETE`; most clients never send it, so under sustained load public sessions accumulate until the pool saturates. Each holds backend pool connections; once the global backend pool hits `MAX_TOTAL_CONNECTIONS` every new connect LRU-evicts a LIVE connection (`evictOneForCapacity`), surfacing as transient `"Failed to re-initialize session ... after backend session loss"` tool failures, with cap-refusals and evictions piling up alongside them. Raising the cap `100 → 400` only bought headroom. The existing age-based `SessionLifetimeManagerImpl` cleanup timer keys off `configService.getSessionLifetime()`, which is null in prod BY DESIGN (persistent sessions never expire), so it never fires — this reaps on a DIFFERENT axis: last-request IDLE time. New isolated module `apps/backend/src/routers/public-metamcp/public-session-sweeper.ts` (`PublicSessionSweeper`), wired into `streamable-http.ts` (the file that owns the public-session registry `sessionManager` and the `cleanupSession` DELETE path). Tracks last-activity per session in a cheap in-memory map (stamped on every request via `markInFlight`/`markSettled` around the dispatch + `beginTracking` at session creation); a periodic sweeper reaps sessions idle beyond `PUBLIC_SESSION_TTL_SECONDS` (default 86400/24h — generous so a long-idle-but-real consumer survives a workday) every `SESSION_SWEEP_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (default 300; 0 disables either knob, no timer) by running `reapIdleSession` — a ROW-PRESERVING teardown variant, NOT the same path a client DELETE uses (see Review round below). A reaped session's consumer reconnects transparently on its next request via the fork's lazy session-recovery path (rebuilds the transport from `mcp_sessions` — verified live). Never reaps a session with an in-flight request (a long tool call, or an open standalone GET stream, that outlives the TTL is live use, not idleness — guarded on an in-flight counter incremented around the dispatch). Conventions mirror PR #70's tools sweep: env-tunable interval, a re-entrancy guard (`sweepInProgress`, cleared in `finally`), cleanup on dispose (`stop()`, wired into `index.ts`'s `gracefulShutdown`), WARN/INFO discipline — one INFO line per sweep ONLY when it reaps (`reaped N idle public session(s) (ttl Xs), released ~M connections`, where M = a best-effort backend active-connection-count delta across the reap batch), debug otherwise. Reap counters (`totalSweeps`/`totalReaped`/`totalConnectionsReleased`/`lastSweepAt`/…) exposed additively on the `/health/sessions` payload. `PUBLIC_SESSION_TTL_SECONDS` + `SESSION_SWEEP_INTERVAL_SECONDS` added to `turbo.json`'s env allowlist. **Scope: StreamableHTTP only** — SSE public sessions already self-reap on socket close (`res.on("close")` in `sse.ts`), so they are not the accumulation vector; the shared `SessionLifetimeManagerImpl` and the age-based `sessionLifetime` path are untouched. **Review round (2026-07-14, verdict MERGE-AFTER-FIXES):** one MAJOR + five smaller findings on the first cut. MAJOR: the sweep reaped via `cleanupSession`, which ALSO deletes the `mcp_sessions` row — a reaped session's next request then 404'd instead of lazy-recovering, and while spec-conformant SDK clients handle that cleanly (re-`initialize`), the Anthropic/claude.ai connector wraps the 404 as `-32600 "Anthropic Proxy: Invalid content from server"` and stays broken until a manual `/mcp reconnect` — the exact failure mode PR #22/#23's capability-hash refusal narrowing exists to avoid. Fix: `cleanupSessionInternal` now takes a `deleteRow` flag; `cleanupSession` (client DELETE, age-based cleanup timer) stays `deleteRow: true`; new `reapIdleSession` (the sweep's own reap path) is `deleteRow: false` — the row survives so `recoverPersistedSession` rebuilds the transport transparently. Accepted tradeoff: reaped rows linger in `mcp_sessions` until the `MCP_SESSION_TTL_DAYS` pruner (default 7d) catches them — rows are tiny and the principal hash still gates recovery, so this is a storage non-issue; a dedicated shorter purge for sweep-reaped rows specifically is a named follow-up, not this PR. Five smaller fixes: (2) `sweepOnce` now reaps candidates SEQUENTIALLY with a recheck (`in-flight == 0 && still idle beyond TTL`) evaluated fresh immediately before each individual reap, not just at snapshot time, so a request landing while an earlier candidate's reap awaits real I/O can't have a later candidate's transport closed under it (tradeoff: a large first-sweep backlog reaps slower than a parallel-fire batch would — correctness over throughput). (3) `touch()`/`markInFlight()`/`markSettled()` are now guarded no-ops for an untracked session, so a trailing call landing after a concurrent `forget()` (client DELETE racing an in-flight request) can't resurrect a zombie tracking entry; new `beginTracking()` is the one unconditional seed, used at session creation and — new — inside `recoverPersistedSession`, so a recovered session resumes TTL tracking instead of falling out of the sweep forever. (4) `index.ts`'s `gracefulShutdown` now also stops the sweeper timer. (5) the reap log line now reads `released ~N` — an explicit best-effort marker, not an exact per-session count. (6) documented the half-open-TCP blind spot on `dispatchTracked`'s doc comment: a client that dies without a clean FIN/RST keeps the in-flight guard held forever for an open standalone GET stream, so that session is never reaped even though nothing is really listening — accepted, not fixed here; `SO_KEEPALIVE` / an SSE heartbeat named as the out-of-scope follow-up. `public-session-sweeper.test.ts`: **40 cases** (was 24 — all originals updated for the guarded touch/markInFlight/markSettled API, seeded via `beginTracking`; +2 for the sequential recheck-before-reap race; +6 for the touch/markInFlight/markSettled no-op-when-untracked guard and `beginTracking` reseed). New `streamable-http.test.ts`: **8 cases** — `reapIdleSession` vs `cleanupSession` row-preservation contract; `recoverPersistedSession` succeeding against a row left in exactly the state a sweep reap leaves it (+ not-found/auth-mismatch contrast cases); `dispatchTracked`'s in-flight wiring held for a simulated open GET stream and released once it settles (+ a throw-releases-in-flight case). | 2026-07-28 | [#84](https://github.com/Umbrella-IT-Group/metamcp/pull/84) | **Endpoint-scoped API keys + `require_scoped_api_key` endpoint gate.** MERGED 2026-07-29 (squash `b0b67a1`); DEPLOYED to prod 2026-07-29 in the #85 image build (`a35db2a`, `/health/upstream` 13/13 healthy — migration `0023` applied, proven transitively by `0024` accepting an acts-as mint on top). Closes the "every API key is gateway-wide" gap: `validateApiKey` is a flat key lookup with no endpoint predicate, so any key reaches every `enable_api_key_auth` endpoint. (1) New nullable `api_keys.endpoint_uuid` FK → `endpoints.uuid` `ON DELETE CASCADE` + index (migration `0023_endpoint_scoped_api_keys`, hand-written idempotent per fork convention, modeled on 0021): non-NULL binds a key to exactly ONE endpoint — `checkApiKeyAccess` (the gate both `authenticateApiKey` branches funnel through; covers streamable-http, SSE, and the OpenAPI bridge) denies it everywhere else. NULL = legacy/unscoped, deliberately GRANDFATHERED per operator ruling: the pre-existing keys keep reaching every endpoint, no forced migration. (2) Mint path: `endpoint_uuid` XOR explicit `all_endpoints: true` REQUIRED (zod `superRefine` on the create form/request schemas + a mirrored impl guard for schema-bypassing callers) — a silent default to global is impossible; scope selection is admin-only in the existing FORBIDDEN-before-write RBAC style (net effect with the mandatory-scope rule: member key minting is closed — pinned by test); the scoped endpoint must exist (NOT_FOUND). `all_endpoints: true` stores NULL. Scope is IMMUTABLE BY OMISSION — neither `UpdateApiKeyRequestSchema` nor the repo update field sets carry `endpoint_uuid` (zod strips a smuggled field; pinned by test); re-scope = delete + re-mint. The internal auto-mint in `endpoints.impl.ts` (createMcpServer convenience) now scopes its key to the endpoint it is minted for; `BOOTSTRAP_API_KEYS` raw inserts stay NULL-scope by design (the grandfathered class). (3) New `endpoints.require_scoped_api_key` (`boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false`, same migration): when set, `checkApiKeyAccess` denies unscoped keys on that endpoint — the opt-out that lets a sensitive endpoint (e.g. m365 before its `enable_api_key_auth` flip in the follow-up PR) refuse grandfathered gateway-wide keys; default false = zero behavior change. (4) UI: key-create dialog gains a REQUIRED endpoint picker with an admin-only "All endpoints (Global)" option; member + admin key lists gain a Scope column (endpoint name; amber "All endpoints" badge for NULL); endpoint create/edit dialogs expose the require-scoped toggle inside the API-key auth section; strings en + mirrored ko/zh. Sequencing note for the m365 follow-up: flip a sensitive endpoint's `enable_api_key_auth` only AFTER its consumer's key is scoped, and set `require_scoped_api_key` on it first. **Migration-ordering note (READ BEFORE MERGING EITHER PR):** held PR #79 also carries a `0023_*` migration. The SECOND PR to land must do BOTH on rebase, not just the first — renumber alone is a silent-skip trap: (a) rename its SQL file + `_journal.json` `tag` to `0024_*`, AND (b) bump that journal entry's `when` (folderMillis) STRICTLY ABOVE the first lander's `0023` `when`. Drizzle (`drizzle-orm/pg-core/dialect.js`) applies only journal entries whose `when` exceeds the max already-applied `when`, so a `0024` that keeps an older `when` is SILENTLY SKIPPED — no error, the table is just never created in prod. This PR's `0023` uses `when: 1785196800000`; a second-landing renumber must exceed that, and after any rebase re-verify the renumbered entry's `when` is the largest in `entries[]`. **Review round 1 (2026-07-29):** two HIGH session findings + one HIGH bootstrap + MED/LOW hardening fixed on this branch before merge — (1) endpoint-binding on the in-memory session lookup (streamable-http + SSE + DELETE guard): a session id is now served only on the endpoint it was created against (`SessionBinding` + `bindingMatches`, mirroring `recoverPersistedSession`'s cross-namespace predicate), closing cross-endpoint session replay; (2) `/health/sessions` (unauthenticated) no longer publishes live session ids — counts + sweeper stats only; (3) the `BOOTSTRAP_RECREATE_USER` + `preserveApiKeysOnRecreate` path now preserves each key's endpoint scope as uuid + endpoint NAME and restores AFTER `bootstrapEndpoints`, re-resolving name → the recreated endpoint's CURRENT uuid (the user delete cascades user-owned endpoints away and they return with fresh uuids, so the preserved uuid is FK-invalid by restore time) — scope survives recreate via NAME re-resolution, not uuid; a scoped key whose endpoint no longer exists is dropped LOUDLY (skipped + counted, never NULL-promoted to gateway-wide) and the restore log reports restored/skipped counts instead of blanket success; (4) `require_scoped_api_key` documented as API-KEY-ONLY (does not gate OAuth — see `checkOAuthAccess`) in UI copy + the tRPC field comment; (5) the createMcpServer auto-mint now reuses only a key that authenticates on the new endpoint (exact-scope, else unscoped) else mints a scoped one; (6) `validate` drops `endpoint_uuid` from its response (key-oracle narrowing); (7) member Create-API-Key UI disabled with an explanatory line (minting is admin-only); (8) endpoint-delete dialog warns bound keys are CASCADE-destroyed. Gates (post-review-round-1): backend vitest **426 passed (43 files)** incl. `api-key-access.test.ts` (13 cases) + extended `api-keys.impl.test.ts` (scope RBAC, xor guard, stores-NULL, NOT_FOUND, update-schema strip) + new `session-lifetime-manager.test.ts` (bindingMatches predicate + binding storage), `streamable-http.test.ts` getBoundSession mismatch-404 + health-payload-no-ids, `bootstrap.preserve.test.ts` (scope survives recreate via name re-resolution; endpoint-gone → skip, never NULL-promote; counts correct), `endpoints.impl.pick-key.test.ts` (auto-mint reuse); `pnpm turbo build` 4/4; `check-types` 2/2; prettier clean on touched code; zero net-new ESLint warnings (backend/frontend max-warnings-0 baselines fail pre-existing on `umbrella`, re-verified via stash runs). **Review round 2 (2026-07-29):** re-review verdict FIX-FIRST — round 1's session-binding work confirmed closed, but its bootstrap fix was FK-broken on the common path: `db.delete(users)` cascades user-owned endpoints away (`endpoints_user_id_users_id_fk ON DELETE cascade`) and `bootstrapEndpoints` recreates them with fresh uuids, so restoring the preserved `endpoint_uuid` violated the `api_keys` FK — swallowed by the catch, with an unconditional "✓ Restored" log (silent credential LOSS reported as success). Fixed: preserve also captures the endpoint NAME (left join); the restore is deferred until after `bootstrapEndpoints` and re-resolves name → current uuid via the pure `planPreservedApiKeyRestores`; a key whose endpoint no longer exists is SKIPPED loudly (never NULL-promoted to gateway-wide) and the log reports restored/skipped/failed counts. Also round 2: the DELETE guard's persisted branch now funnels through `bindingMatches` (single security predicate, no drift); the SSE `/message` guard and the streamable DELETE guard (incl. `findById` branch + lookup-failure fallback) extracted into exported pure resolvers (`resolveSseMessageSession`, `resolveDeletableSession`) with unit tests — match / cross-endpoint / absent / DB-error-treated-as-absent / indistinguishable 404 shaping; the three remaining `getSessionIds()` request-path log dumps removed (count-only, one demoted to debug); the api-keys page resolves the session role (`roleLoaded` + `.catch`) before claiming key-create is admin-only (neutral disabled button until then). | 2026-07-29 | [#85](https://github.com/Umbrella-IT-Group/metamcp/pull/85) | **API-key-bound identity (acts-as) for m365 delegated injection.** MERGED 2026-07-29 (squash `a35db2a`); DEPLOYED to prod 2026-07-29 ~06:07Z (push build run 30426814937 success; acts-as mint accepted on prod → migration `0024` live; first consumer: the sever endpoint's scoped+bound key, 16 tools verified end-to-end from LXC 205). Makes an API key an EXPLICIT, admin-controlled identity source for the per-user Graph-token injection — the "one teammate's key exercises another identity" hazard `docs/mcp-auth.md` warns about, shipped safely because it is PAIRED with #84's endpoint scoping. (1) New nullable `api_keys.acts_as_user_id` `text` FK → `users.id` `ON DELETE CASCADE` + index (migration `0024_api_key_acts_as_identity`, hand-written idempotent; journal `when` 1785283200000 > 0023's 1785196800000 per the migration-ordering note above — re-verify largest-in-entries after any rebase). NULL (every existing key) = no identity, injection keeps fail-closing. (2) The ONE streamable-http m365 context gate (`handleRequestWithUserContext`) gains an `api_key` branch: `authMethod === "api_key" && apiKeyActsAsUserId` → `{ userId }`; OAuth branch unchanged and takes precedence. `validateApiKey` projects the binding; `authenticateApiKey` stamps `apiKeyActsAsUserId` on BOTH its api-key branches. (3) Mint policy (zod superRefine + mirrored impl guard for schema-bypassing callers): binding is admin-only (FORBIDDEN-before-write), REQUIRES a non-null `endpoint_uuid` (rejected with `all_endpoints: true` or missing scope — an identity-bound key must never be gateway-wide; this pairing IS the safety argument), target user must exist (friendly NOT_FOUND via new minimal `usersRepository.findById`; deliberately no list-users tRPC). Creation-only / IMMUTABLE BY OMISSION from every update schema + repo update method (zod strips a smuggled field; pinned by test). (4) Fail-closed by design and PINNED BY TEST: unbound keys inject nothing (a key never "acts as its creator"); SSE + OpenAPI bridge have NO m365 context wiring (source-level absence test); `validate` still discloses neither scope nor binding. (5) Audit: `resolveClientIdentity` labels a bound key `key-name (as user-email)` — a string extension of the #39/#57 identity label, same shape for all consumers. **AUDIT LABEL-SHAPE NOTE for downstream query owners:** `tool_call_audit.client_name` (and Live Logs `clientName`) now carries `key (as email)` for identity-bound keys — an external dashboard grouping by EXACT `client_name` sees a bound key's label change the moment it is bound (and again within ~60s of an acted-as email change), and "every call acting as X" requires a `LIKE '%(as x@y)%'` match, not equality. A structured `acts_as_user_id` column on `tool_call_audit` is the right long-term shape — deliberately NOT added in #85 (schema churn on a hot audit table deserves its own change); noted here as a future line. Round-2 behavior: the immutable binding (name + acts_as id) is cached per key uuid while the mutable email is composed per call behind a 60s TTL, and a DB error reading the key row labels the caller `api-key <short> (identity unresolved)` UNCACHED — never a bare label indistinguishable from a no-identity key. (6) UI: admin-only "Acts as user" free-text input (better-auth user id) in the create dialog, enabled ONLY when a specific endpoint is selected AND ownership is a specific user, cleared on both the all-endpoints switch and the 'everyone' ownership switch (free text stays because the ownership select carries no concrete user ids and there is deliberately no list-users tRPC — the backend ownership invariant enforces regardless); identity badge in member (short id) + admin (email, via aliased second users join) key lists; strings en/ko/zh. **Review round 2 (2026-07-29), all findings fixed:** (a) OWNERSHIP INVARIANT [high] — an identity-bound key must be OWNED by the identity it exercises: FORBIDDEN in the impl (before any DB read) when the effective owner ≠ `acts_as_user_id`, mirrored in both zod superRefines (`ownerMismatch`, en/ko/zh), killing the public-('everyone')-owner combination (public keys' raw values are listed to every member — a public identity-bound key would be a fleet-distributed delegated Graph credential) and the foreign-owner one; admin-owned acting-as-self and owned-by-the-acted-as-user still pass. (b) UI gating [high] as in (6). (c) RUNTIME pairing re-check [medium] — both `authenticateApiKey` branches stamp via `resolveActsAsUserId()`: acts-as honored only when `endpoint_uuid` is also non-null, so an unscoped-but-bound row written outside the app (psql/admin_cli) can never become a gateway-wide identity key. (d) STRUCTURAL invariant [medium] — migration 0024 adds `api_keys_acts_as_requires_scope` CHECK (`acts_as_user_id IS NULL OR endpoint_uuid IS NOT NULL`), DO-block-guarded for idempotency; mirrored in schema.ts via drizzle `check()`. (e) BOOTSTRAP preserve/restore [medium] — the recreate cycle now captures `acts_as_user_id` + the acted-as EMAIL and re-resolves email → current user id at restore (sign-up mints a fresh id, same reasoning as the endpoint-name pair); an unresolvable identity SKIPS the key loudly (never restored unbound = silent degradation); capture widened to `or(user_id, acts_as_user_id)` so foreign-owned/public keys bound to the recreated user (killed by the acts_as CASCADE) are counted in a loud warn instead of dying silently. (f)+(g) resolver label fixes [low] as in (5). Sequencing unchanged from #84: bind + scope the consumer's key FIRST, flip the m365 endpoint's `enable_api_key_auth` LAST, with `require_scoped_api_key` set.
